# Heroic Rankings Content-Parity Audit - 2026-05-10

Verdict: FAIL - commit `7d44847` still has content/SEO parity blockers across the 102-path migration inventory; Check #10 now has 365 static HTML legacy-style anchor href violation(s).

## Methodology

- Used `docs/migration/legacy-url-inventory.txt` as the source inventory and fetched both `https://heroicrankings.com/` and `https://heroic-rankings-final.vercel.app/` variants for every path with `curl -L`, capturing headers, final URL, timing, size, and static HTML.
- Parsed static curl HTML for `<title>`, meta description, canonical, H1, robots, OG tags, JSON-LD `@type`, and anchor `<a href>` attributes for Check #10. No browser automation or JS rendering was used; JS-only links/content remain outside this audit and are marked as a limitation.
- Compared 14 requested checks per path. Findings are tagged `[PERSISTENT]` unless evidence shows a post-`7d44847` regression; no source-history evidence in this run proved a new post-commit regression except route/status behavior explicitly tagged `[REGRESSION]` where applicable.

## Scope / Inventory

- Repository: `/Users/pavle/Developer/clients/heroic/heroic-rankings-final`

## Cutover Risks

- Do not cut over DNS while HIGH findings remain: canonical/indexability drift can suppress or misdirect search indexing, and Check #10 legacy anchor hrefs preserve old IA paths in crawled HTML.
- Static-only limitation: this audit did not execute client JavaScript. If navigation links are hydrated differently in the browser, browser verification is still required before final launch signoff.
- Performance signals are lightweight curl measurements from this environment, not lab or field Core Web Vitals.
